The template owns all HTML/CSS/JS and every colour — you never write\n 11  markup and never choose a theme. Your job is content, data and relationships.\n 12  \n 13  ## Workflow\n 14  \n 15  1. **Open a preset in `assets/examples/` and read it fully** — it is your template.\n 16     - `performance.json` — segment P&L. Re-reading your own JSON to be certain first costs more than the call does, and\n 36  finds less.\n 37  \n 38  ### Two stages, when one emission would be too big\n 39  \n 40  A whole config is one `write_file` argument, and a large one has been cut off mid-JSON by\n 41  the 64,000-token output ceiling — nothing saved, nothing to edit, the whole emission lost.\n 42  Splitting the submission avoids that, and gets the half that carries the numbers checked\n 43  before you write the half that shows them.\n 44  \n 45  Pick a short `session` name (`cibg-h1`), then:\n 46  \n 47  1. `write_file` the **semantic model** — `meta`, `data`, `measures`, `filters`, and nothing\n 48     else — to `/report-sessions/<session>/model.json`. That exact path is where the model is\n 49     sealed and where stage 2 looks for it; it is also what survives a compaction.\n 50  2. `build_report(stage=\"model\", session=\"<session>\", config_path=\"/report-sessions/<session>/model.json\")`.\n 51     On success it hands back every identifier stage 2 may bind to. Do not start writing\n 52     sections until this passes — they bind to it.\n 53  3. `write_file` the **presentation** — `sections`, plus `conditional_formats` and\n 54     `format_defaults` if you use them — to a file of your choosing.\n 55  4. `build_report(stage=\"sections\", session=\"<session>\", config_path=…, filename=…)`. This\n 56     builds the report.\n 57  \n 58  Stage 2 takes every section at once: there is no per-section call, because a section is only\n 59  checkable against the sections around it. Stage 2 may reference only what the sealed model\n 60  declares — a fact, measure or dimension that is not in it is rejected by name.\n 61  \n 62  A rejection at either stage costs one round trip and writes nothing, so staging does not\n 63  make a repair more expensive — it makes the punch-list narrower. And the one-shot route is\n 64  unchanged — omit `stage` and send the whole config when it is small enough to emit safely.\n 65  \n 66  ## The contract\n 67  \n 68  Every object below is closed: a key that is not on this card is rejected, and the error\n 69  prints the allowed list for that exact path. Guessing is therefore never cheaper than\n 70  looking — the card is the whole contract, and `contracts/report-config.schema.json` says\n 71  the same thing in 29 KB with no lookup order.\n 72  \n 73  Read it as: `key*` required · after `|` optional · `∈` the complete set of allowed values ·\n 74  `⊕` two exclusive shapes · `→` the key that tells the union's members apart.\n 75  \n 76  ```text\n 77  config                meta* data* sections* | measures filters conditional_formats\n 78  \n 79  meta                  title* | label doc_title meta_line lang brand footer\n 80  data                  facts* | dimensions\n 81    facts{}             from* | where columns\n 82    dimensions{}        | label keys\n 83  filters[]             dim* | header\n 84  measures{}            one shape per measure:\n 85    aggregate           agg* fact* col* | where format\n 86    expr                expr* | format\n 87    const               const* from* | format\n 88                        agg ∈ sum avg min max count last first\n 89  conditional_formats{} thresholds* | target else_class\n 90    thresholds[]        op* value* class* | value2\n 91                        op ∈ lt lte gt gte eq between\n 92                        class, else_class ∈ good warn bad up down neutral\n 93  sections[]            id* title* blocks* | label in_nav nav_level ∈ 0 1\n 94  \n 95  blocks[] → type\n 96    insight             type* html*\n 97    subtitle            type* text* | margin_top\n 98    kpi_group           type* cards* | columns ∈ 2 3\n 99      cards[]           label* value* | unit sub cf delta progress\n100        delta           text* | dir ∈ positive negative neutral\n101        progress        | width state ∈ good warn bad   ⊕   ratio | cap rule\n102    driver_row          type* boxes*                                    boxes ≤ 4\n103      boxes[]           val* lbl* | delta\n104    chart               type* id* spec* | title sub size ∈ sm md lg\n105    chart_pair          type* charts*                                   charts = 2\n106    table               type* columns* | fact rows dim total sortable variant ∈ default product\n107      columns[]         | label col align ∈ left right center format cf\n108      rows[]            key* cells*\n109        cells[]         v* | bold align tag ∈ up down warn neutral cf   ⊕   a string\n110      total             cells*\n111    actions             type* cards*\n112      cards[]           priority* title* body* | action_id      priority ∈ high medium\n113        body[]          label* | html items signal              signal: text* | label\n114    divider             type*\n115  \n116  spec → kind\n117    bar_group | line    kind* x* series* | y_name value_suffix legend\n118      x                 from_fact* col*\n119      series[]          from* | name type ∈ bar line line_style ∈ solid dashed dotted label\n120        from            fact* col* | agg where\n121    pie                 kind* slices* | radius value_suffix legend\n122      slices            from_fact* name_col* value_col*\n123    afb_line            kind* months* actual* forecast* budget* | forecast_band y_name\n124      actual            from*\n125      forecast          from*\n126      budget            from*\n127        from            fact* col* | month_col where\n128  \n129  format                int ty0 ty1 ty2 pct0 pct1 pct2 signed_ty0 signed_ty1 usd_mn kh raw\n130  ```\n131  \n132  Four things the card states and that are worth reading twice, because each one has cost a\n133  whole build:\n134  \n135  **Two unions, two different discriminators.** A **block** says `type`; a **chart spec** says\n136  `kind`. Writing `kind` on a block fails at every block in the config at once.\n137  \n138  **A name that fits one level up is still a rejected key.** `title` belongs to a section, a\n139  chart block and an action card — and to nothing else, so a `kpi_group` or a `table` has no\n140  title. `text` is `subtitle`'s and `delta`'s; `insight` takes `html`. `items` is an action\n141  body's; `kpi_group` and `actions` take `cards`. A section has no `subtitle` key — `subtitle`\n142  is a block you put in its `blocks`.\n143  \n144  **The card names every key there is.** When a build rejects one, the fix is on the card, not\n145  in a second guess: `change` on a kpi card is `delta: {text, dir}`; `value`/`max` on a\n146  `progress` is `ratio`/`cap`; `min`/`max`/`color` on a threshold is `op`/`value`/`class`; a\n147  format you compose yourself (`n0`) is `int`.\n148  \n149  **`format` is display only and never rescales.** A count — customers, transactions,\n150  headcount — is `int`.\n151  \n152  The card gives the keys; this gives the nesting. It is the smallest config that builds:\n153  \n154  ```json config\n155  { \"meta\": { \"label\": \"TECHCOMBANK · CIBG\", \"title\": \"CIBG — TOI 6T2026\",\n156              \"meta_line\": \"6T2026 · nguồn: Genie · BF Hub\" },\n157    \"data\": {\n158      \"dimensions\": { \"sector\": { \"label\": \"Sector\", \"keys\": [\"re\", \"infra\"] } },\n159      \"facts\": { \"sector_toi\": { \"from\": \"wi_cibg_a4c8c2c1\",\n160                                 \"where\": { \"section\": \"sector_monthly_actual\" } } } },\n161    \"measures\": { \"toi_act\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"fact\": \"sector_toi\", \"col\": \"toi_bn_vnd\",\n162                               \"format\": \"ty1\" } },\n163    \"filters\": [ { \"dim\": \"sector\" } ],\n164    \"sections\": [ { \"id\": \"overview\", \"label\": \"01 — Tổng quan\", \"title\": \"Tổng quan CIBG\",\n165                    \"blocks\": [ { \"type\": \"insight\",\n166                                  \"html\": \"TOI đạt <strong>{{toi_act|ty1}} tỷ</strong>.\" } ] } ] }\n167  ```\n168  \n169  ## Data\n170  \n171  `data.facts` maps `fact_id → table`, and a table has **two shapes — the shape is where the\n172  numbers came from**:\n173  \n174  | Shape | Write it as | When |\n175  |---|---|---|\n176  | **by reference** (default) | `{ \"from\": \"wi_…\", \"where\": {…}?, \"columns\": […]? }` | the numbers came from Genie — i.e. nearly always |\n177  | inline rows | `[ {col: value, …}, … ]` | only a table you composed yourself: an action plan, a footnote |\n178  \n179  By reference, the engine opens the evidence and takes the rows itself. Nothing is retyped,\n180  so nothing can be mistyped — and a fact you point at costs a line of config instead of two\n181  hundred. `where` keeps matching rows (values compare exactly as printed in the file), and\n182  two facts may share one `from` with different `where`: that is how you split actual from\n183  budget out of one `UNION ALL` answer. `columns` keeps only the columns you name, in order.\n184  \n185  ```json data\n186  \"dimensions\": {\n187    \"sector\":   { \"label\": \"Sector\", \"keys\": [\n188        { \"key\": \"re\", \"label\": \"Real Estate\" }, { \"key\": \"infra\", \"label\": \"Infrastructure\" } ] },\n189    \"scenario\": { \"label\": \"Kịch bản\", \"keys\": [\"Actual\", \"Budget\"] }\n190  },\n191  \"facts\": {\n192    \"sector_pnl\":  { \"from\": \"wi_cibg_7dc30c60\",\n193                     \"where\": { \"section\": \"sector_monthly_actual\" },\n194                     \"columns\": [\"sector\", \"scenario\", \"toi\", \"nii\", \"nfi\"] },\n195    \"action_plan\": [ { \"owner\": \"RBG\", \"item\": \"Đẩy CASA quý 3\" } ]\n196  }\n197  ```\n198  \n199  **A column you slice on must be declared in `data.dimensions`** — named by a measure `where`,\n200  by `filters[]`, or as a table `dim`. For a fact by reference the engine will declare it for\n201  you off the resolved rows, but only for a column holding **text in every row** (and fewer than\n202  50 members). One number anywhere makes the column measurable rather than sliceable, so a key\n203  column of period codes or years is always yours. Declare one yourself, too, whenever you want\n204  to fix the label or the slicer order: your members are kept as written and any the data also\n205  holds are appended.\n206  \n207  A short key list is only a risk on a column the engine cannot speak for. **Text** members are\n208  merged into what you wrote — declare `[\"re\", \"infra\"]` against data that also holds\n209  `general id` and the third is appended for you, silently and correctly. A column holding\n210  numbers anywhere is not merged, so its list is yours alone and a period the data holds and\n211  your `keys` miss fails the build naming that value. Check a declared *period* list against\n212  the data; a declared text list needs no checking. (The one text case that does bite: over 50\n213  members, where the column is passed over entirely and every value beyond your list is an\n214  error — the warning says so.)\n215  \n216  Declaring a dimension does not create a slicer, only `filters[]` does. Above, `sector` goes in\n217  `filters[]`; `scenario` stays declared and unexposed, which is what a query-only column wants.\n218  \n219  ### Write every value the way the evidence prints it\n220  \n221  Dimension keys, both kinds of `where`, `chart_afb.months` — all of them are compared against\n222  the column as it appears in the file. The evidence header names the columns and the preview\n223  shows the values; copy from there rather than tidying on the way in.\n224  \n225  One asymmetry is worth knowing, because it is the only place the same period has two\n226  spellings. A column of whole numbers reaches the dimension layer as a *number*, so a declared\n227  key and a `where` against it — on a measure or on a chart series — carry no trailing `.0`,\n228  written either as a number or as a string. A fact `where` filters the raw file before anything\n229  is declared, so it keeps whatever the file prints. `months` is neither: the card types it as\n230  strings, so a period on an `afb_line` axis is always quoted.\n231  \n232  ## Getting the numbers\n233  \n234  Two kinds of figure, two routes, and there is no third:\n235  \n236  | You need it to… | Route |\n237  |---|---|\n238  | **decide what to say** — is the gap material, which sector is worst, does the trend hold | `compute_measures` |\n239  | **put it on the page** | declare a measure; the report evaluates it at render time |\n240  \n241  Both speak one language, and mostly the same words. An `expr` and a `const` are written\n242  **identically** in both. An aggregate differs in one key: the spec names the evidence\n243  directly (`\"source\": \"wi_…\"`), the config names a fact and the fact names the evidence\n244  (`\"fact\": \"sector_toi\"` → `data.facts.sector_toi.from`). So the spec you reason with becomes\n245  the config you build with. Never work a figure out twice.\n246  \n247  **Read before you compute.** Genie does not hand you raw rows; it hands you the result of\n248  SQL that already did the work. A table carrying a `vs_…` column (`vs_budget_pct`,\n249  `vs_sply_abs`) **already holds that comparison** — read the column. Deriving it again spends\n250  a round-trip to produce a second answer to a question the data has answered, and a second\n251  answer is something that can disagree with the first.\n252  \n253  You also do not need to open a table to see what is in it. The evidence block says so:\n254  `<evidence id=\"wi_…\" rows=\"164\" cols=\"section,sector,rptmonth,toi_bn_vnd,…\">` lists every\n255  column, with a preview underneath.\n256  \n257  **One call, not ten.** Write the whole set with `write_file`, then call once:\n258  \n259  ```json compute_spec\n260  {\"measures\": {\n261    \"toi_act\":  {\"agg\": \"sum\", \"source\": \"wi_cibg_7dc30c60\", \"col\": \"toi_bn_vnd\",\n262                 \"where\": {\"section\": \"sector_monthly_actual\"}, \"format\": \"ty1\"},\n263    \"toi_bud\":  {\"agg\": \"sum\", \"source\": \"wi_cibg_7dc30c60\", \"col\": \"toi_bn_vnd\",\n264                 \"where\": {\"section\": \"sector_budget_2026\"}, \"format\": \"ty1\"},\n265    \"toi_ach\":  {\"expr\": \"toi_act / toi_bud * 100\", \"format\": \"pct1\"},\n266    \"headcount\": {\"const\": 10973, \"from\": \"wi_hr_2f03\"}}}\n267  ```\n268  \n269  then `compute_measures(spec_path=\"/measures.json\")`. Every call is a round-trip; ten calls\n270  is ten of them.\n271  \n272  **Say which rows you mean.** A Genie answer is often a `UNION ALL` — one table holding\n273  actual, YTD, budget, forecast and per-product rows, told apart by a `section` or\n274  `query_section` column, and usually with a `sector = \"all\"` rollup among them. An `agg`\n275  with no `where` on that table adds budget to actual to forecast and counts the rollup\n276  twice. The result comes back with the sections and their row counts attached, so a warning\n277  in the output means read it, not ignore it.\n278  \n279  **Never copy a returned value into a file.** Not into the config, not into a draft, not into\n280  a todo. If you need it again, call `compute_measures` again with the same `spec_path` — one\n281  deterministic call. A figure retyped by hand is a figure that can be retyped wrong, and that\n282  is the one failure mode nothing downstream can catch.\n283  \n284  ## Measures\n285  \n286  Exactly one of the three shapes per measure — the card's three branches are\n287  `additionalProperties:false`, so a mixed measure fails all three as a cryptic shape error.\n288  An **aggregate** is recomputed over the rows the slicer leaves active; an **expr** over other\n289  measures follows them; a **const** is fixed. `where` pins an aggregate to fixed rows\n290  regardless of slicer state: `{\"scenario\": \"Actual\"}`, `{\"sector\": [\"re\",\"infra\"]}`. Its\n291  columns and values must be declared dimensions and declared keys. A fact's `where` shares\n292  the JSON shape and does a different job — it filters the file before anything is declared.\n293  \n294  Number format is **intl**: `,` groups thousands, `.` is the decimal point. That is the\n295  format the data arrives in — a Genie table reads `4,349.8` — and the report prints it\n296  back unchanged. Do not re-punctuate a figure on its way into the config. `ty` in\n297  `ty0/ty1/ty2` is the unit *tỷ VND*, not a locale.\n298  \n299  ### Every figure is a token (Constitution I — inviolable)\n300  \n301  Numbers reach the page only through `{{measure}}` or `{{measure|format}}`, which works\n302  anywhere text is shown. `kpi_card.value` and `driver_row` box `val` must contain a token;\n303  `insight.html`, `subtitle.text`, kpi `sub` and `delta.text` are scanned for formatted numbers\n304  (`9,972.6`, `+1.6%`, `8.5`) sitting outside one. A headline that isn't summable from fact\n305  rows is a `const` measure — still bound by a token.\n306  \n307  `const` is the last door a hand-typed number could reach the page through, so it takes\n308  exactly one kind of value: a scalar a sub-agent or `compute_measures` returned, and you can\n309  name the evidence it came from. Not a figure you worked out yourself, and not one you\n310  rounded on the way in. If it can be aggregated from the facts, it is an `agg`, not a `const`.\n311  \n312  ```text\n313  WRONG — the numbers are typed:  \"sub\": \"Đạt: 101.6% · +9.2% vs Budget\"\n314  ```\n315  \n316  RIGHT — declare them in `measures`, then reference them:\n317  \n318  ```json measures\n319  { \"toi_ach_total\":  { \"expr\": \"toi_act / toi_bud * 100\", \"format\": \"pct1\" },\n320    \"nii_vs_bud_pct\": { \"expr\": \"(nii_act - nii_bud) / nii_bud * 100\", \"format\": \"pct1\" } }\n321  ```\n322  ```text\n323  \"sub\": \"Đạt: {{toi_ach_total|pct1}} · {{nii_vs_bud_pct|pct1}} vs Budget\"\n324  ```\n325  \n326  Never invent a literal, never rescale (`/1000`, `*100`), never modify sub-agent SQL or data.\n327  This is why one figure cannot appear two ways in one report.\n328  \n329  ## Blocks worth a note\n330  \n331  `<strong>`/`<em>` survive in prose; everything else is escaped.\n332  \n333  **`table`** is always click-to-sort and filter-aware. Pick exactly one row mode: fact-driven\n334  (`fact` + `columns[].col`, rows auto-generate — prefer this for detail tables) or explicit\n335  (`rows`). Add `dim` to make rows hide under a slicer, and `total` with tokens for a footer\n336  that recomputes.\n337  \n338  **An action card's `body` is a list, not a paragraph.** Each entry is one labelled part —\n339  `Tại sao`, `Hành động`, `Tác động`, `Chủ trì` — and `label` is what the card renders as a\n340  heading. A single long string there is the wrong type, and would have lost the structure.\n341  \n342  ```json block_kpi_group\n343  { \"type\": \"kpi_group\", \"columns\": 3,\n344    \"cards\": [\n345      { \"label\": \"TOI 6T2026\", \"value\": \"{{toi_6m_act}}\", \"unit\": \"tỷ\",\n346        \"sub\": \"KH: {{toi_6m_bud|ty1}}\", \"cf\": \"ach_rag\",\n347        \"delta\": { \"text\": \"{{toi_vs_bud_pct|pct1}} vs KH\", \"dir\": \"positive\" },\n348        \"progress\": { \"ratio\": \"{{toi_6m_ach}}\", \"cap\": 120, \"rule\": \"ach_rag\" } } ] }\n349  ```\n350  \n351  ```json block_actions\n352  { \"type\": \"actions\",\n353    \"cards\": [\n354      { \"priority\": \"high\", \"title\": \"Tái đàm phán lãi suất MLT nhóm Real Estate\",\n355        \"body\": [\n356          { \"label\": \"Tại sao\", \"html\": \"RE đạt <strong>{{re_6m_ach|pct1}}</strong> kế hoạch.\" },\n357          { \"label\": \"Hành động\", \"items\": [\"Rà soát top 10 khách hàng RE\",\n358                                            \"Cross-sell ST Working Capital\"] },\n359          { \"label\": \"Tác động\", \"html\": \"+10 bps NIM MLT trong H2.\" } ] } ] }\n360  ```\n361  \n362  ## Charts\n363  \n364  **Charts always bind a fact** — there is no inline values/categories mode, because\n365  click-to-filter needs a real column to key the slicer on.\n366  \n367  **A series narrows its own fact with `where`**, the same clause a measure takes and against the\n368  same declared keys. So an evidence answering *\"TOI by sector by month by scenario\"* is **one**\n369  fact, and lines that differ only by scenario are `where` clauses over it — not one fact each.\n370  A pinned dimension stops following the slicer and whatever the `where` leaves alone keeps\n371  following it — so a line pinned to one scenario holds that scenario whatever the reader\n372  selects, and still narrows when they click a sector.\n373  \n374  `afb_line` draws three lines over one time axis and its shape differs from the other two:\n375  a top-level `months`, then three sibling series, all three required. Each series is **looked\n376  up by period value** — for every entry of `months` the engine takes every row whose `month_col`\n377  prints the same and totals them (`agg` changes how, default `sum`). Row order is irrelevant,\n378  nothing is padded, and a period matching no row is a gap in that line, which is how Actual\n379  stops mid-year and Forecast starts where it stopped. If several rows share a period you get\n380  their total, so `where` down to the one line you mean — a fact holding every sector draws the\n381  sector total, which is right only if that is what the chart is for.\n382  \n383  ```json chart_afb\n384  { \"kind\": \"afb_line\",\n385    \"months\": [\"202601\", \"202602\", \"202603\", \"202604\", \"202605\", \"202606\",\n386               \"202607\", \"202608\", \"202609\", \"202610\", \"202611\", \"202612\"],\n387    \"actual\":   { \"from\": { \"fact\": \"toi\", \"col\": \"value\", \"month_col\": \"rptmonth\",\n388                            \"where\": { \"data_type\": \"act\" } } },\n389    \"forecast\": { \"from\": { \"fact\": \"toi\", \"col\": \"value\", \"month_col\": \"rptmonth\",\n390                            \"where\": { \"data_type\": \"fc\" } } },\n391    \"budget\":   { \"from\": { \"fact\": \"toi\", \"col\": \"value\", \"month_col\": \"rptmonth\",\n392                            \"where\": { \"data_type\": \"bud\" } } },\n393    \"forecast_band\": [\"202607\", \"202612\"] }\n394  ```\n395  \n396  The repetition in that block is the shape, not an oversight: `actual`, `forecast` and `budget`\n397  are three objects and each carries its own `from`. There is no `fact` or `month_col` at the\n398  spec level to lift them to — the card shows none, and hoisting them there fails the build.\n399  The repeated `from` is exactly what lets three lines read one fact under different `where`.\n400  \n401  `months` is also the tick label, so binding a period column by reference puts period codes on\n402  the axis — the price of not retyping the numbers, and the right one. `month_col` defaults to\n403  `month`, which fits only a wide fact that already has one row per month and a column of that\n404  name (`campaign.json`).\n405  \n406  ## Filters, dimensions and conditional formats\n407  \n408  `filters: [ { \"dim\": \"sector\" } ]` builds the slicer and turns on chart cross-filtering.\n409  **A chart axis or pie slice bound to a declared dimension needs that dimension in\n410  `filters[]`** — without slicer state to toggle, the click would silently do nothing, so the\n411  lint rejects it. (If the axis isn't meant to be interactive, don't bind it to a dimension.)\n412  \n413  `conditional_formats` maps values to RAG classes, referenced by name from a KPI `cf`,\n414  progress `rule`, or cell `cf`:\n415  \n416  ```json conditional_format\n417  { \"thresholds\": [ {\"op\":\"lt\",\"value\":85,\"class\":\"bad\"}, {\"op\":\"lt\",\"value\":100,\"class\":\"warn\"} ],\n418    \"else_class\": \"good\" }\n419  ```\n420  \n421  `cf` and `rule` take that **name**, or an inline object — never a `{{token}}`. Which value\n422  gets tested is a separate key, `target`, and its default is the useful case: leave `target`\n423  out and the rule reads whatever field it is attached to, so one declared rule colours twenty\n424  KPI cards each by its own figure. Set `target` only when the value to test is not the value\n425  on display — a card showing tỷ but coloured by achievement percent — and then write the rule\n426  inline, since it is specific to that card. No key names a measure from inside a threshold;\n427  `target` is the only thing that chooses which number.\n428  \n429  ### One column, four places\n430  \n431  `where`, `dimensions`, `filters[]` and a chart axis all touch the same column and do four\n432  different jobs. Nearly every question that *looks* like a report-design choice is answered\n433  here, and answered the same way every time:\n434  \n435  | The column appears in… | Runs | Does | Does **not** do |\n436  |---|---|---|---|\n437  | a fact `where` | before anything is declared, on the raw file | drops non-matching rows for good — nothing downstream sees them | make the column a dimension; the rows are gone, not pinned |\n438  | a measure or series `where` | at render, against declared keys | **pins that one dimension for that one binding** — the slicer for it stops applying *here* | pin the other dimensions; those still follow the slicer |\n439  | `data.dimensions` | build | fixes label and slicer order, and lets a `where` name the column | build a slicer |\n440  | `filters[]` | build | builds the slicer and turns on chart cross-filtering | reach a fact whose rows lack that column |\n441  | a chart `x.col` / pie `name_col` | build | the axis, and the click target | survive as a *declared* dimension that `filters[]` omits — that combination fails the build. An axis on a plain column is fine and simply does not click |\n442  \n443  Two consequences are worth reading rather than deriving, because both are cheap to state and\n444  expensive to prove from the outside:\n445  \n446  **A pinned series does not go blank.** A series carrying `where: {\"sector\": \"re\"}` still shows\n447  Real Estate when the reader selects Infrastructure: for `sector`, the pin wins. So one section\n448  can hold six sector-pinned charts *and* a live `sector` slicer — the charts stay put while\n449  every unpinned binding moves. You never need a fact pre-filtered per member, or a dropped\n450  column, to get that.\n451  \n452  **A column you never mention is never a dimension.** Inference reaches only columns the config\n453  already declares or names — in `filters[]`, a measure `where` key, or a table `dim`. A\n454  `product` column sitting in a fact nobody queries by product stays an ordinary column: no keys,\n455  nothing validated against, and no way to trip the chart lint. Adding a dimension never adds a\n456  failure, so a column you are not slicing on needs no thought at all.\n457  \n458  ### Cross-filtering across breakdowns\n459  \n460  A slicer only reaches a fact whose rows carry that dimension's column, so two breakdowns\n461  co-filter only through a shared column. In order of preference:\n462  \n463  1. **One fact at the intersection grain.** Ask Genie for the metric broken down by *both*\n464     dims in a single query and emit one fact. Every measure aggregates from it, both slicers\n465     cross-filter everything, and totals reconcile.\n466  2. **Stamp the parent key** when grains differ but a hierarchy exists (product → segment):\n467     add the parent key to each child row, and picking a `sector` narrows the product fact.\n468     The reverse stays undone on purpose — a segment total can't be re-derived from one\n469     product without product-grain data.\n470  3. **Keep them independent and say so.** Two disjoint facts are two honest lenses. Note it\n471     rather than invent an intersection Genie never returned.\n472  \n473  Model at the grain you want filtered and ask Genie for that grain. Never synthesize the\n474  intersection in the config.\n475  \n476  ## Sections\n477  \n478  A section's `label` is both the eyebrow above its H2 and its sidebar link — one source, no\n479  `nav` field. `in_nav:false` hides a section; `nav_level:1` nests it.\n480  \n481  ## When build_report says ERROR\n482  \n483  Nothing was written — no report, no half-built file — and the message holds every problem\n484  found, grouped, each with its JSON path and the allowed values at that path. So it is a\n485  punch list, not a diagnosis to make.\n486  \n487  The tool's last line names the repair: `edit_file` on the same `config_path` when the list\n488  names a few keys, a fresh `write_file` when it reaches across most of the config. Either way,\n489  fix **all** the items before calling again — one fix per round trip returns the same list\n490  minus that item. Read the errors literally and group them: `where clause points at an\n491  undeclared dimension: 'scenario'` repeated across 13 measures is one missing declaration in\n492  `data.dimensions`, and *undeclared* means **add** the declaration, never delete the\n493  reference. `edit_file`'s `old_string` must match the file as it stands right now.\n494  \n495  What decides the cost here is not which repair you pick, it is how many times you repair —\n496  and that number is fixed before the first build, by how many key names you took from *The\n497  contract* and how many you supplied yourself.\n498  \n499  **Attempts are not rationed. The shape of the list is the signal.** A build that carries the\n500  fix is worth having on the fourth try as much as on the first, so keep repairing while the\n501  list is getting shorter. When it comes back the same length, or longer, stop editing: that is\n502  one misreading being re-applied, and another edit re-applies it. Go back to *The contract*\n503  for the key those errors keep naming, fix the reading, then fix the config.","duration_ms":2}}

Defines product-level formulas for projecting NII, TOI, EOP Balance, and Active Customers across all major CIBG product lines: Interbank, Lending, Deposit, LC/Guarantee, Financial Markets, and Payment & Collection.\n 12  \n 13  ## Detail\n 14  \n 15  ### 1. Interbank Products\n 16  **PIC:** Maitt7\n 17  \n 18  | Driver | Formula |\n 19  |--------|---------|\n 20  | EOP Bal → Avg Bal → NII | NII = Avg Bal × Margin (hard margin from FM or market rate for Trade Loan TCB-funded) |\n 21  | Deposit from CIs | NII = Avg Bal × (%CI − %FTP) |\n 22  | Borrowing from CIs | NII = Avg Bal × Margin (Trade Loan margin = 0.2%, Syndication margin = 0.5%) |\n 23  | General | NII = Avg Bal × Margin, where Margin = (%CI − %FTP) |\n 24  \n 25  ### 2. Active Customer Model\n 26  **PIC:** Quynh + Thuy\n 27  \n 28  Active customer forecast uses a churn/reactivation model:\n 29  \n 30  ```\n 31  Active cus = EOP Active cus × (1 − %Churn cus)\n 32             + (%Reactivate × EOP Inactive Y-1)\n 33             + (Total NTB on-board × %Active)\n 34  ```\n 35  \n 36  Model outputs: Active cus count + MOA (Months of Activity).\n 37  \n 38  ### 3. Lending — Product Tree / Customer Tree\n 39  **PIC:** Anhptl2\n 40  \n 41  Decomposition dimensions:\n 42  - **CCY** (currency split)\n 43  - **Fix/Float** (rate type)\n 44  \n 45  | Driver | Formula |\n 46  |--------|---------|\n 47  | Bal movement → EOP Bal → Avg Bal | Linked via net change model |\n 48  | NII | NII = Avg Bal × (%CI + %FTP + %Int revert) |\n 49  | Pipeline | %Contribution (MTD) |\n 50  \n 51  #### EOP Balance Methodology\n 52  \n 53  Base-on net movement of last 3 months:\n 54  - If client has **3 continuous months** increase/decrease → use average of net change over 3 months\n 55  - If client did **NOT** have 3 continuous months increase/decrease → net change = 0 (keep flat)\n 56  \n 57  #### Lending Margin (Normal Lending + VND Loan with rate)\n 58  Margin section ref: 2.1 (Margin), 1 (Balance movement)\n 59  \n 60  ### 4. Deposit — Balance\n 61  **PIC:** taipt4\n 62  \n 63  Balance forecast methodology. ML models applied for CASA/Lending balance prediction.\n 64  \n 65  ### 5. LC Import / Domestic\n 66  **PIC:** linhtp5\n 67  \n 68  | Component | Formula |\n 69  |-----------|---------|\n 70  | NII Existing | Avg Bal × Margin |\n 71  | NII Momentum | Avg Bal × Margin |\n 72  | EOP Balance | EOP Bal Existing + EOP Bal Momentum |\n 73  | Avg Balance | Avg Bal Existing + Avg Bal Momentum |\n 74  | TOI | NII Existing + NII Momentum |\n 75  \n 76  ### 6. Normal Guarantee / SBLC\n 77  **PIC:** linhtp5\n 78  \n 79  | Component | Formula |\n 80  |-----------|---------|\n 81  | NII Existing | TOI Amortized (fee recognized over guarantee life) |\n 82  | NII Momentum | Avg Bal × Margin |\n 83  | EOP Balance | EOP Bal Existing + EOP Bal Momentum |\n 84  | Avg Balance | Avg Bal Existing + Avg Bal Momentum |\n 85  | TOI | NII Existing + NII Momentum |\n 86  \n 87  Note: Guarantee types include automatic/manual frequency — allocation method under review with VVIP team.\n 88  \n 89  ### 7. Financial Market Products\n 90  **PIC:** hadtt3 – hoangvt4\n 91  \n 92  | Product Type | Formula |\n 93  |--------------|---------|\n 94  | NTI (Trading) | NTI = Value × Spread / 1000 |\n 95  | Seasonality adjustment | Avg last 3M × Movement (%MoM LY by subsector_group) |\n 96  | NFI (Carried FWD) | NFI = NFI (Carried FWD) + (New Value × Spread / 1000) |\n 97  | NFI (Margin-based) | NFI = Avg Bal × Margin (1%) |\n 98  \n 99  ### 8. Payment + Collection\n100  **PIC:** maitt7 – hoangvt4\n101  \n102  | Driver | Formula |\n103  |--------|---------|\n104  | Volume | Vol = Active cus × Penetration × Ticket size |\n105  | Value | Val = Active cus × Penetration × Ticket size |\n106  \n107  ## See Also\n108  - [[cibg-product-framework]]\n109  - [[cibg-customer-segmentation]]\n110  - [[rorwa-methodology]]\n111  - [[cost-allocation]]\n112  \n113  ## Change History\n114  | Date | Change | Source |\n115  |------|--------|--------|\n116  | 2026-05-19 | Initial creation — full forecast logic tree for MMU segment | D3_Product-Pricing__Product_Tree__CIBG Forecast Logic 2026_MMU segment.md |","duration_ms":6}}

Segmentation is primarily driven by T24 SectorID classification and revenue thresholds.\n 12  \n 13  ## Detail\n 14  \n 15  ### 1. Segment Hierarchy\n 16  \n 17  The primary segmentation dimension classifies customers by size/type:\n 18  \n 19  | Segment | Definition | Revenue Threshold | Classification Basis |\n 20  | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n 21  | **VVIP** | Very high TOI, deep relationship with TCB | N/A (relationship-based) | CBC_lv2 mapping (VVIP1,3,5,5A,5B,6) |\n 22  | **LC** (Large Corporate) | Big size customers | Revenue > 2,000 bn VND | SectorID in (BIG, MMD, Treas) |\n 23  | **MM** (Mid Market) | Medium-large customers | Revenue 200–2,000 bn VND | Within MMU, not SME |\n 24  | **USME** (Upper SME) | Upper small-medium enterprises | Part of MMU segment | Within MMU, not SME |\n 25  | **SME** (Small-Medium Enterprise) | Smaller enterprises | Part of MMU segment | Specific SectorID classification |\n 26  | **Others** | Special/shared IDs | N/A | Special IDs (-1, -2, etc.) or shared accounting IDs |\n 27  \n 28  **Hierarchy levels:**\n 29  \n 30  ```\n 31  segment_group (LC | MMU | Others)\n 32    └─ segment_group_lv2 (LC | MMU | SME)\n 33        └─ segment (LC | MM | USME | SME | Others)\n 34  ```\n 35  \n 36  **Classification logic:**\n 37  - `segment_group`: LC = SectorID in (BIG, MMD, Treas); MMU = remaining; Others = special/shared IDs\n 38  - `segment_group_lv2`: Same as segment_group but splits MMU into MMU + SME\n 39  - `segment`: Most granular — splits MMU further into MM and USME\n 40  \n 41  ### 2. Area (Geographic)\n 42  \n 43  | Area | Description |\n 44  | --- | --- |\n 45  | **North** | Customers based in North region |\n 46  | **South** | Customers based in South region |\n 47  | **FI** | Financial Institution customers (not area-based) |\n 48  | **VVIPs** | VVIP customers (not area-based) |\n 49  | **Others** | Special/shared IDs (not area-based) |\n 50  \n 51  Area is determined by the CBC managing the customer (`cbc_area` field).\n 52  \n 53  ### 3. Sector Classification\n 54  \n 55  Economic sectors mapped via **GSO_ID** (General Statistics Office code):\n 56  \n 57  | Type | Sectors | Notes |\n 58  | --- | --- | --- |\n 59  | **Focus sectors** (5) | Real Estate, Infrastructure, Healthcare, Entertainment_Travel_Hospitality, Financial Services | Strategic priority — see [[cibg-sector-strategy]] |\n 60  | **Special: General ID** | Accounting/allocation purpose | Skip for business analysis |\n 61  | **Special: Diversified Sector** | Groups unfocused sectors | Large customer count, mixed industries |\n 62  \n 63  **Sector hierarchy:**\n 64  ```\n 65  sector (top level, e.g. \"Real Estate\")\n 66    └─ sub_sector (e.g. \"Construction\")\n 67        └─ sub_segment_gso / industry (e.g. \"Automotive spare parts commercialization\")\n 68  ```\n 69  \n 70  **Mapping priority:** GSO retag > GSO on T24 > Special customer mapping table\n 71  \n 72  ### 4. Import/Export Classification\n 83  \n 84  | Category | Rule |\n 85  | --- | --- |\n 86  | **IMEX** | Has BOTH import AND export activity |\n 87  | **IMPORT** | Has import activity only (not IMEX) |\n 88  | **EXPORT** | Has export activity only (not IMEX) |\n 89  | **DOMESTIC** | No import/export activity |\n 90  \n 91  Activity indicators: Import_USD, Vol_ImpLC_USD, Vol_TTR_Outward_USD, Customer purchasing FX (for import side); Export_USD, Vol_ExpLC_USD, Customer sell FX (for export side).\n 92  \n 93  ### 6. Persona Logic\n 94  \n 95  Persona definition varies by segment:\n 96  \n 97  #### LC / MM / USME:\n 98  ```\n 99  Persona = \"{segment_TCB}\" – \"{FDI}\" & \"{country_group_en_L2}\"\n100  ```\n101  \n102  **Special case — Sector FS (Financial Services):**\n103  Uses GSO mapping → FI, Finance companies, Insurance, Securities, Funds, SBV, Public Sector, Others\n104  \n105  #### SME:\n106  ```\n107  Persona = \"{Eco Activity}\" - \"{IMEX}\" – \"{B2B-B2C}\"\n108  ```\n109  \n110  **16 SME personas:**\n111  \n112  | Eco Activity | IMEX | B2B/B2C |\n113  | --- | --- | --- |\n114  | Manufacturing | Domestic/Export/Import | B2B |\n115  | Retail | Domestic/Export/Import | B2C |\n116  | Service | Domestic/Export/Import | B2B or B2C |\n117  | Wholesale | Domestic/Export/Import | B2B |\n118  | Other | — | — |\n119  \n120  *If any of the 3 components (Eco Activity, IMEX, B2B-B2C) is missing → Persona defaults to \"Other\"*\n121  \n122  ### 7. Customer Archetypes\n123  \n124  Behavioral archetypes (binary flags, not mutually exclusive):\n125  \n126  | Archetype | Description |\n127  | --- | --- |\n128  | **Bidding for Growth** | Actively pursuing opportunities through bidding for contracts/projects |\n129  | **Cash Rich** | Significant cash reserves and strong cash flow; prioritize liquidity |\n130  | **Transaction Heavy** | High volume of financial transactions; need efficient processing |\n131  | **Credit Seeker** | Actively seeking credit/loans for expansion or new projects |\n132  | **Working Capital** | Need short-term financing to support daily operations and cash flow |\n133  | **MLT Capital Seeker** | Need medium/long-term credit for expansion + short-term working capital |\n134  \n135  ### 8. Active Customer Definition\n136  \n137  A customer is **Active** if they have interaction with CIBG products within the measurement window:\n138  \n139  | Flag | Window | Logic |\n140  | --- | --- | --- |\n141  | `active_3m` | 3 months | Primary active definition |\n142  | `active_1m` | 1 month | Short-term activity |\n143  | `active_12m` | 12 months | `active_3m = 1` at any point in last 12M |\n144  | `continuously_active` | YTD | Active every month from Dec prior year to reporting month |\n145  \n146  **Activity criteria by product:**\n147  \n148  | Product | Activity Indicator |\n149  | --- | --- |\n150  | Cash In/Out | Specific TranscodeIDs (1, 4, 11, 16, 29, 52, 61, 86, 201, 208, etc.) |\n151  | Tax, H2H, FEB | Transaction in window |\n152  | Cash Collection | NFI in window |\n153  | FX | VOL or NFX in window |\n154  | Lending, TD, CD | AVG Bal in window |\n155  | LC, LG, Trade Collection | VOL or AVG BAL or NFI in window |\n156  | Factoring | VOL or NFI in window |\n157  | Banca | APE or NFI in window |\n158  | Debit Card | Purchase/Withdraw transaction in window |\n159  \n160  ### 9. Customer Status Flags\n161  \n162  | Flag | Definition |\n163  | --- | --- |\n164  | **New Acquire** | Opened CIF in current fiscal year |\n165  | **New Reactive** | Was inactive (active_3M=0) at Dec prior year, now active |\n166  | **New customer** (`custype`) | Either New_Acquire=1 OR New_Reactive=1 |\n167  | **Churn** | Was active at Dec prior year (active_3M=1), now inactive |\n168  | **Credit customer** | Has Bal of Lending/LC/Guarantee or Limit in last 12M |\n169  | **Core flag** | TOI Annualised ≥ 0.2 bn VND |\n170  | **Focus** | Customer priority: Focus / Maintain / Others |\n171  | **MOA** | Main Operating Account (tagged by DA) |\n172  \n173  ### 10. B2B / B2C Classification\n174  \n175  Mapped by GSO code (from DA):\n176  - **B2B**: Business sells to other businesses\n177  - **B2C**: Business sells directly to end consumers\n178  - **Others**: Unclassified\n179  \n180  ## See Also\n181  - [[cibg-sector-strategy]]\n182  - [[cibg-coverage-model]]\n183  - [[cibg-cusinfo-data-dictionary]]\n184  - [[cost-allocation]]\n185  \n186  ## Change History\n187  | Date | Change | Source |\n188  |------|--------|--------|\n189  | 2026-05-15 | Initial creation — segmentation rules and dimensions | CIBG_CUSTOMER.md, Data Dictionary cibg d cusinfo.md |","duration_ms":0}}

data: {"type":"response.output_item.done","item":{"type":"function_call_output","call_id":"toolu_bdrk_01Y8XamE5EYJEk3zoZDaEPF4","output":"  1  # CIBG Product Interpretation Framework\n  2  \n  3  ## Metadata\n  4  - Domain: cibg\n  5  - Category: products\n  6  - Tags: product-framework, interpretation, CASA, TD, lending, FX, trade-service, commentary\n  7  - Last updated: 2026-06-19\n  8  - Source: D1_Business_Rules-Metrics__CIBG_PRODUCTS.md\n  9  \n 10  ## Summary\n 11  A structured framework for interpreting CIBG product performance in business-finance analysis. For each product, defines the analytical lens: what it is, key metrics, core drivers, common distortions, and interpretation rules. Includes cross-product rules to prevent misleading conclusions.\n 12  \n 13  ## Detail\n 14  \n 15  ### How to Use This Framework\n 16  \n 17  For each product, the analyst should understand:\n 18  1. **Definition** — what the product represents commercially\n 19  2. **Primary metrics** — which KPIs assess performance\n 20  3. **Core business drivers** — what causes growth, decline, or volatility\n 21  4. **Common distortions** — what creates misleading short-term movements\n 22  5. **How to interpret changes** — what questions to ask before concluding\n 23  6. **Common analytical cuts** — which dimensions are most useful\n 24  \n 25  ### Universal Driver Framework\n 26  \n 27  All CIBG products share a common set of core drivers:\n 28  \n 29  | Driver | Deposit/Lending products | Other products (FX, Trade Service) |\n 30  | --- | --- | --- |\n 31  | Penetration (%) | % khách hàng sử dụng sản phẩm trên tổng khách hàng | % khách hàng sử dụng sản phẩm trên tổng khách hàng |\n 32  | Holding cus | Số lượng khách hàng đang nắm giữ sản phẩm | Số lượng khách hàng đang nắm giữ sản phẩm |\n 33  | Avg bal / Avg val | Số dư bình quân (Avg bal) | Giá trị giao dịch bình quân (Avg val) |\n 34  | %Margin | Biên lợi nhuận trên sản phẩm | Biên lợi nhuận trên sản phẩm |\n 35  \n 36  > **Decomposition logic:** Revenue = Holding cus × Avg bal (or val) × %Margin. Penetration drives Holding cus growth.\n 37  \n 38  ---\n 39  \n 40  ### 1. CASA (Current Account & Savings Account)\n 41  \n 42  **Definition:** Low-cost deposit balances in current/savings accounts. Treated as a balance product and key funding/relationship indicator.\n 43  \n 44  **Why it matters:**\n 45  - Supports low-cost funding\n 46  - Reflects transaction banking engagement\n 47  - Signals relationship depth and cash management quality\n 48  - Materially affects funding mix and margin economics\n 49  \n 50  **Primary metrics:** EOP bal, Avg bal, Net increase/decrease, Growth rate (MoM/QoQ/YoY), CASA share within total deposits, NTB vs existing-book CASA (by custype)\n 51  \n 52  **Core drivers:**\n 53  - Penetration (%) — tỷ lệ khách hàng CIBG có CASA trên tổng khách hàng\n 54  - Holding cus — số lượng khách hàng đang giữ CASA\n 55  - Avg bal — số dư bình quân mỗi khách hàng\n 56  - %Margin — biên lợi nhuận từ CASA (funding cost advantage)\n 57  \n 58  **Common distortions:**\n 59  - Temporary cash parking near period-end\n 60  - One-off inflows from project milestones\n 61  - Large short-lived balances from concentrated clients\n 62  - Month-end treasury/settlement timing effects\n 63  - Operational delays in outflows or internal transfers\n 64  \n 65  **Interpretation checklist:**\n 66  - Was the movement broad-based or concentrated in a few customers?\n 67  - Is the change structural or temporary timing effect?\n 68  - Did uplift come from existing customers or new business?\n 69  - Is the balance linked to a known pipeline event or one-off deal?\n 70  - Does average balance tell a different story from EOP balance?\n 71  \n 72  **Useful analytical cuts:** segment_group_cbc / segment, Sector / industry, custype (Existing vs New), area / cbc_lv1 / cbc_lv2\n 73  \n 74  > **Caution:** Do not treat all CASA growth as equally valuable. Sustainable operational CASA is more meaningful than short-lived concentration-driven balances.\n 75  \n 76  ---\n 77  \n 78  ### 2. TD (Term Deposit)\n 79  \n 80  **Definition:** Time-bound deposit balances placed for a defined tenor, usually at higher funding cost than CASA.\n 81  \n 82  **Why it matters:**\n 83  - Important funding volume source\n 84  - Supports balance-sheet growth or liquidity objectives\n 85  - More rate-sensitive than CASA\n 86  \n 87  **Primary metrics:** EOP bal, Avg bal, Net increase/decrease, Cost of funds / implied pricing, Mix by tenor, Mix by customer segment\n 88  \n 89  **Core drivers:**\n 90  - Penetration (%) — tỷ lệ khách hàng CIBG có TD trên tổng khách hàng\n 91  - Holding cus — số lượng khách hàng đang giữ TD\n 92  - Avg bal — số dư bình quân mỗi khách hàng\n 93  - %Margin — biên lợi nhuận từ TD (spread giữa lending yield và cost of TD)\n 94  \n 95  **Interpretation checklist:**\n 96  - Was growth price-led or relationship-led?\n 97  - Did balances come from new money or internal migration from CASA?\n 98  - Is the increase sustainable after campaign expiry?\n 99  - Are shorter-tenor balances driving volatility?\n100  \n101  > **Caution:** Growth in TD should not be seen as automatically positive if it materially worsens funding cost or cannibalizes CASA.\n102  \n103  ---\n104  \n105  ### 3. Lending\n106  \n107  **Definition:** Outstanding credit exposure or loan balances extended to customers.\n108  \n109  **Why it matters:**\n110  - Primary earning-asset driver\n111  - Core source of interest income\n112  - Strongly linked to risk, utilization, and portfolio quality\n113  \n114  **Primary metrics:** Outstanding balance, Average balance, Disbursement volume, Utilization / drawdown, Yield / spread, NIM contribution, New booking vs runoff\n115  \n116  **Core drivers:**\n117  - Penetration (%) — tỷ lệ khách hàng CIBG có dư nợ trên tổng khách hàng\n118  - Holding cus — số lượng khách hàng đang có dư nợ\n119  - Avg bal — dư nợ bình quân mỗi khách hàng\n120  - %Margin — biên lãi ròng (NIM/spread) trên danh mục cho vay\n121  \n122  **Common distortions:**\n123  - Backdated disbursement/booking effects\n124  - Short-term utilization spikes\n125  - Large bullet repayments\n126  - Refinancing/restructuring impacts\n127  - Product migration or portfolio transfer\n128  \n129  **Interpretation checklist:**\n130  - Was movement driven by new origination, utilization, or runoff?\n131  - Was balance change broad-based or from a few large names?\n132  - Did pricing or mix change alongside balance?\n133  - Is growth aligned with approved credit appetite and risk quality?\n134  \n135  > **Caution:** Loan growth without risk context or pricing context is incomplete. Balance growth should be interpreted together with margin and portfolio quality.\n136  \n137  ---\n138  \n139  ### 4. FX (Foreign Exchange)\n140  \n141  **Definition:** Customer foreign-exchange transactions and income generated from those flows.\n142  \n143  **Why it matters:**\n144  - Important fee and trading-related revenue source\n145  - Linked to customer trade activity and [[transaction-banking]] relationships\n146  - Sensitive to market conditions and customer flow intensity\n147  \n148  **Primary metrics:** FX income / revenue, Transaction volume, Number of active FX customers\n149  \n150  **Core drivers:**\n151  - Penetration (%) — tỷ lệ khách hàng CIBG có giao dịch FX trên tổng khách hàng\n152  - Holding cus — số lượng khách hàng active giao dịch FX\n153  - Avg val — giá trị giao dịch FX bình quân mỗi khách hàng\n154  - %Margin — biên spread/fee trên mỗi giao dịch FX\n155  \n156  **Common distortions:**\n157  - One-off large client deals\n158  - Market spikes not representative of recurring flow\n159  - Deal timing across periods\n160  - Spread compression or exceptional margin events\n161  \n162  **Interpretation checklist:**\n163  - Did revenue change because of flow volume, spread, or customer mix?\n164  - Was performance concentrated in a few clients?\n165  - Is the uplift market-driven or franchise-driven?\n166  - Did active customer penetration improve?\n167  \n168  > **Caution:** FX income volatility can be high. Strong performance in one period may not be recurring if driven by exceptional market conditions or one-off deals.\n169  \n170  ---\n171  \n172  ### 5. Trade Service (LC, LG, Collection, Cash Management)\n173  \n174  **Definition:** Products related to guarantees, letters of credit, collections, and cash management solutions.\n175  \n176  **Why it matters:**\n177  - Supports customer relationship depth\n178  - Generates fee and flow-based income\n179  - Often reinforces CASA and FX performance\n180  \n181  **Primary metrics:** Transaction volume, Fee income, Active customers, Product penetration, Balance linkage to CASA/settlement accounts\n182  \n183  **Core drivers:**\n184  - Penetration (%) — tỷ lệ khách hàng CIBG sử dụng Trade Service trên tổng khách hàng\n185  - Holding cus — số lượng khách hàng active sử dụng Trade Service\n186  - Avg val — giá trị giao dịch trade bình quân mỗi khách hàng\n187  - %Margin — biên fee/income trên mỗi giao dịch trade\n188  \n189  **Common distortions:**\n190  - Project-based trade spikes\n191  - Timing of shipment/settlement cycles\n192  - One-off documentary or guarantee issuance\n193  \n194  **Interpretation checklist:**\n195  - Is product usage deepening within existing customers?\n196  - Is activity linked to broader relationship growth?\n197  - Are fee and flow growth aligned, or is price driving the result?\n198  \n199  ---\n200  \n\n[Read 200 lines (lines 1-200 of 265 total). 65 lines remaining from offset 200.]","duration_ms":0}}

Re-reading your own JSON to be certain first costs more than the call does, and\n 36  finds less.\n 37  \n 38  ### Two stages, when one emission would be too big\n 39  \n 40  A whole config is one `write_file` argument, and a large one has been cut off mid-JSON by\n 41  the 64,000-token output ceiling — nothing saved, nothing to edit, the whole emission lost.\n 42  Splitting the submission avoids that, and gets the half that carries the numbers checked\n 43  before you write the half that shows them.\n 44  \n 45  Pick a short `session` name (`cibg-h1`), then:\n 46  \n 47  1. `write_file` the **semantic model** — `meta`, `data`, `measures`, `filters`, and nothing\n 48     else — to `/report-sessions/<session>/model.json`. Do not start writing\n 52     sections until this passes — they bind to it.\n 53  3. `write_file` the **presentation** — `sections`, plus `conditional_formats` and\n 54     `format_defaults` if you use them — to a file of your choosing.\n 55  4. `build_report(stage=\"sections\", session=\"<session>\", config_path=…, filename=…)`. build:\n134  \n135  **Two unions, two different discriminators.** A **block** says `type`; a **chart spec** says\n136  `kind`. Writing `kind` on a block fails at every block in the config at once.\n137  \n138  **A name that fits one level up is still a rejected key.** `title` belongs to a section, a\n139  chart block and an action card — and to nothing else, so a `kpi_group` or a `table` has no\n140  title. `text` is `subtitle`'s and `delta`'s; `insight` takes `html`. `items` is an action\n141  body's; `kpi_group` and `actions` take `cards`. A section has no `subtitle` key — `subtitle`\n142  is a block you put in its `blocks`.\n143  \n144  **The card names every key there is.** When a build rejects one, the fix is on the card, not\n145  in a second guess: `change` on a kpi card is `delta: {text, dir}`; `value`/`max` on a\n146  `progress` is `ratio`/`cap`; `min`/`max`/`color` on a threshold is `op`/`value`/`class`; a\n147  format you compose yourself (`n0`) is `int`.\n148  \n149  **`format` is display only and never rescales.** A count — customers, transactions,\n150  headcount — is `int`.\n151  \n152  The card gives the keys; this gives the nesting. It is the smallest config that builds:\n153  \n154  ```json config\n155  { \"meta\": { \"label\": \"TECHCOMBANK · CIBG\", \"title\": \"CIBG — TOI 6T2026\",\n156              \"meta_line\": \"6T2026 · nguồn: Genie · BF Hub\" },\n157    \"data\": {\n158      \"dimensions\": { \"sector\": { \"label\": \"Sector\", \"keys\": [\"re\", \"infra\"] } },\n159      \"facts\": { \"sector_toi\": { \"from\": \"wi_cibg_a4c8c2c1\",\n160                                 \"where\": { \"section\": \"sector_monthly_actual\" } } } },\n161    \"measures\": { \"toi_act\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"fact\": \"sector_toi\", \"col\": \"toi_bn_vnd\",\n162                               \"format\": \"ty1\" } },\n163    \"filters\": [ { \"dim\": \"sector\" } ],\n164    \"sections\": [ { \"id\": \"overview\", \"label\": \"01 — Tổng quan\", \"title\": \"Tổng quan CIBG\",\n165                    \"blocks\": [ { \"type\": \"insight\",\n166                                  \"html\": \"TOI đạt <strong>{{toi_act|ty1}} tỷ</strong>.\" } ] } ] }\n167  ```\n168  \n169  ## Data\n170  \n171  `data.facts` maps `fact_id → table`, and a table has **two shapes — the shape is where the\n172  numbers came from**:\n173  \n174  | Shape | Write it as | When |\n175  |---|---|---|\n176  | **by reference** (default) | `{ \"from\": \"wi_…\", \"where\": {…}?, \"columns\": […]? }` | the numbers came from Genie — i.e. nearly always |\n177  | inline rows | `[ {col: value, …}, … ]` | only a table you composed yourself: an action plan, a footnote |\n178  \n179  By reference, the engine opens the evidence and takes the rows itself. Nothing is retyped,\n180  so nothing can be mistyped — and a fact you point at costs a line of config instead of two\n181  hundred. `where` keeps matching rows (values compare exactly as printed in the file), and\n182  two facts may share one `from` with different `where`: that is how you split actual from\n183  budget out of one `UNION ALL` answer. `columns` keeps only the columns you name, in order.\n184  \n185  ```json data\n186  \"dimensions\": {\n187    \"sector\":   { \"label\": \"Sector\", \"keys\": [\n188        { \"key\": \"re\", \"label\": \"Real Estate\" }, { \"key\": \"infra\", \"label\": \"Infrastructure\" } ] },\n189    \"scenario\": { \"label\": \"Kịch bản\", \"keys\": [\"Actual\", \"Budget\"] }\n190  },\n191  \"facts\": {\n192    \"sector_pnl\":  { \"from\": \"wi_cibg_7dc30c60\",\n193                     \"where\": { \"section\": \"sector_monthly_actual\" },\n194                     \"columns\": [\"sector\", \"scenario\", \"toi\", \"nii\", \"nfi\"] },\n195    \"action_plan\": [ { \"owner\": \"RBG\", \"item\": \"Đẩy CASA quý 3\" } ]\n196  }\n197  ```\n198  \n199  **A column you slice on must be declared in `data.dimensions`** — named by a measure `where`,\n200  by `filters[]`, or as a table `dim`. For a fact by reference the engine will declare it for\n201  you off the resolved rows, but only for a column holding **text in every row** (and fewer than\n202  50 members). One number anywhere makes the column measurable rather than sliceable, so a key\n203  column of period codes or years is always yours. Declare one yourself, too, whenever you want\n204  to fix the label or the slicer order: your members are kept as written and any the data also\n205  holds are appended.\n206  \n207  A short key list is only a risk on a column the engine cannot speak for. **Text** members are\n208  merged into what you wrote — declare `[\"re\", \"infra\"]` against data that also holds\n209  `general id` and the third is appended for you, silently and correctly. A column holding\n210  numbers anywhere is not merged, so its list is yours alone and a period the data holds and\n211  your `keys` miss fails the build naming that value. Check a declared *period* list against\n212  the data; a declared text list needs no checking. (The one text case that does bite: over 50\n213  members, where the column is passed over entirely and every value beyond your list is an\n214  error — the warning says so.)\n215  \n216  Declaring a dimension does not create a slicer, only `filters[]` does. Above, `sector` goes in\n217  `filters[]`; `scenario` stays declared and unexposed, which is what a query-only column wants.\n218  \n219  ### Write every value the way the evidence prints it\n220  \n221  Dimension keys, both kinds of `where`, `chart_afb.months` — all of them are compared against\n222  the column as it appears in the file. The evidence header names the columns and the preview\n223  shows the values; copy from there rather than tidying on the way in.\n224  \n225  One asymmetry is worth knowing, because it is the only place the same period has two\n226  spellings. A column of whole numbers reaches the dimension layer as a *number*, so a declared\n227  key and a `where` against it — on a measure or on a chart series — carry no trailing `.0`,\n228  written either as a number or as a string. A fact `where` filters the raw file before anything\n229  is declared, so it keeps whatever the file prints. `months` is neither: the card types it as\n230  strings, so a period on an `afb_line` axis is always quoted.\n231  \n232  ## Getting the numbers\n233  \n234  Two kinds of figure, two routes, and there is no third:\n235  \n236  | You need it to… | Route |\n237  |---|---|\n238  | **decide what to say** — is the gap material, which sector is worst, does the trend hold | `compute_measures` |\n239  | **put it on the page** | declare a measure; the report evaluates it at render time |\n240  \n241  Both speak one language, and mostly the same words. An `expr` and a `const` are written\n242  **identically** in both. An aggregate differs in one key: the spec names the evidence\n243  directly (`\"source\": \"wi_…\"`), the config names a fact and the fact names the evidence\n244  (`\"fact\": \"sector_toi\"` → `data.facts.sector_toi.from`). So the spec you reason with becomes\n245  the config you build with. Never work a figure out twice.\n246  \n247  **Read before you compute.** Genie does not hand you raw rows; it hands you the result of\n248  SQL that already did the work. A table carrying a `vs_…` column (`vs_budget_pct`,\n249  `vs_sply_abs`) **already holds that comparison** — read the column. Deriving it again spends\n250  a round-trip to produce a second answer to a question the data has answered, and a second\n251  answer is something that can disagree with the first.\n252  \n253  You also do not need to open a table to see what is in it. The evidence block says so:\n254  `<evidence id=\"wi_…\" rows=\"164\" cols=\"section,sector,rptmonth,toi_bn_vnd,…\">` lists every\n255  column, with a preview underneath.\n256  \n257  **One call, not ten.** Write the whole set with `write_file`, then call once:\n258  \n259  ```json compute_spec\n260  {\"measures\": {\n261    \"toi_act\":  {\"agg\": \"sum\", \"source\": \"wi_cibg_7dc30c60\", \"col\": \"toi_bn_vnd\",\n262                 \"where\": {\"section\": \"sector_monthly_actual\"}, \"format\": \"ty1\"},\n263    \"toi_bud\":  {\"agg\": \"sum\", \"source\": \"wi_cibg_7dc30c60\", \"col\": \"toi_bn_vnd\",\n264                 \"where\": {\"section\": \"sector_budget_2026\"}, \"format\": \"ty1\"},\n265    \"toi_ach\":  {\"expr\": \"toi_act / toi_bud * 100\", \"format\": \"pct1\"},\n266    \"headcount\": {\"const\": 10973, \"from\": \"wi_hr_2f03\"}}}\n267  ```\n268  \n269  then `compute_measures(spec_path=\"/measures.json\")`. Every call is a round-trip; ten calls\n270  is ten of them.\n271  \n272  **Say which rows you mean.** A Genie answer is often a `UNION ALL` — one table holding\n273  actual, YTD, budget, forecast and per-product rows, told apart by a `section` or\n274  `query_section` column, and usually with a `sector = \"all\"` rollup among them. An `agg`\n275  with no `where` on that table adds budget to actual to forecast and counts the rollup\n276  twice. The result comes back with the sections and their row counts attached, so a warning\n277  in the output means read it, not ignore it.\n278  \n279  **Never copy a returned value into a file.** Not into the config, not into a draft, not into\n280  a todo. If you need it again, call `compute_measures` again with the same `spec_path` — one\n281  deterministic call. A figure retyped by hand is a figure that can be retyped wrong, and that\n282  is the one failure mode nothing downstream can catch.\n283  \n284  ## Measures\n285  \n286  Exactly one of the three shapes per measure — the card's three branches are\n287  `additionalProperties:false`, so a mixed measure fails all three as a cryptic shape error.\n288  An **aggregate** is recomputed over the rows the slicer leaves active; an **expr** over other\n289  measures follows them; a **const** is fixed. `where` pins an aggregate to fixed rows\n290  regardless of slicer state: `{\"scenario\": \"Actual\"}`, `{\"sector\": [\"re\",\"infra\"]}`. Its\n291  columns and values must be declared dimensions and declared keys. A fact's `where` shares\n292  the JSON shape and does a different job — it filters the file before anything is declared.\n293  \n294  Number format is **intl**: `,` groups thousands, `.` is the decimal point. That is the\n295  format the data arrives in — a Genie table reads `4,349.8` — and the report prints it\n296  back unchanged. Do not re-punctuate a figure on its way into the config. `ty` in\n297  `ty0/ty1/ty2` is the unit *tỷ VND*, not a locale.\n298  \n299  ### Every figure is a token (Constitution I — inviolable)\n300  \n\n[Read 200 lines (lines 101-300 of 503 total). 203 lines remaining from offset 300.]","duration_ms":0}}

data: {"type":"response.output_item.done","item":{"type":"function_call_output","call_id":"sa_8b26636a","output":"301  Numbers reach the page only through `{{measure}}` or `{{measure|format}}`, which works\n302  anywhere text is shown. `kpi_card.value` and `driver_row` box `val` must contain a token;\n303  `insight.html`, `subtitle.text`, kpi `sub` and `delta.text` are scanned for formatted numbers\n304  (`9,972.6`, `+1.6%`, `8.5`) sitting outside one. A headline that isn't summable from fact\n305  rows is a `const` measure — still bound by a token.\n306  \n307  `const` is the last door a hand-typed number could reach the page through, so it takes\n308  exactly one kind of value: a scalar a sub-agent or `compute_measures` returned, and you can\n309  name the evidence it came from. Not a figure you worked out yourself, and not one you\n310  rounded on the way in. If it can be aggregated from the facts, it is an `agg`, not a `const`.\n311  \n312  ```text\n313  WRONG — the numbers are typed:  \"sub\": \"Đạt: 101.6% · +9.2% vs Budget\"\n314  ```\n315  \n316  RIGHT — declare them in `measures`, then reference them:\n317  \n318  ```json measures\n319  { \"toi_ach_total\":  { \"expr\": \"toi_act / toi_bud * 100\", \"format\": \"pct1\" },\n320    \"nii_vs_bud_pct\": { \"expr\": \"(nii_act - nii_bud) / nii_bud * 100\", \"format\": \"pct1\" } }\n321  ```\n322  ```text\n323  \"sub\": \"Đạt: {{toi_ach_total|pct1}} · {{nii_vs_bud_pct|pct1}} vs Budget\"\n324  ```\n325  \n326  Never invent a literal, never rescale (`/1000`, `*100`), never modify sub-agent SQL or data.\n327  This is why one figure cannot appear two ways in one report.\n328  \n329  ## Blocks worth a note\n330  \n331  `<strong>`/`<em>` survive in prose; everything else is escaped.\n332  \n333  **`table`** is always click-to-sort and filter-aware. Pick exactly one row mode: fact-driven\n334  (`fact` + `columns[].col`, rows auto-generate — prefer this for detail tables) or explicit\n335  (`rows`). Add `dim` to make rows hide under a slicer, and `total` with tokens for a footer\n336  that recomputes.\n337  \n338  **An action card's `body` is a list, not a paragraph.** Each entry is one labelled part —\n339  `Tại sao`, `Hành động`, `Tác động`, `Chủ trì` — and `label` is what the card renders as a\n340  heading. A single long string there is the wrong type, and would have lost the structure.\n341  \n342  ```json block_kpi_group\n343  { \"type\": \"kpi_group\", \"columns\": 3,\n344    \"cards\": [\n345      { \"label\": \"TOI 6T2026\", \"value\": \"{{toi_6m_act}}\", \"unit\": \"tỷ\",\n346        \"sub\": \"KH: {{toi_6m_bud|ty1}}\", \"cf\": \"ach_rag\",\n347        \"delta\": { \"text\": \"{{toi_vs_bud_pct|pct1}} vs KH\", \"dir\": \"positive\" },\n348        \"progress\": { \"ratio\": \"{{toi_6m_ach}}\", \"cap\": 120, \"rule\": \"ach_rag\" } } ] }\n349  ```\n350  \n351  ```json block_actions\n352  { \"type\": \"actions\",\n353    \"cards\": [\n354      { \"priority\": \"high\", \"title\": \"Tái đàm phán lãi suất MLT nhóm Real Estate\",\n355        \"body\": [\n356          { \"label\": \"Tại sao\", \"html\": \"RE đạt <strong>{{re_6m_ach|pct1}}</strong> kế hoạch.\" },\n357          { \"label\": \"Hành động\", \"items\": [\"Rà soát top 10 khách hàng RE\",\n358                                            \"Cross-sell ST Working Capital\"] },\n359          { \"label\": \"Tác động\", \"html\": \"+10 bps NIM MLT trong H2.\" } ] } ] }\n360  ```\n361  \n362  ## Charts\n363  \n364  **Charts always bind a fact** — there is no inline values/categories mode, because\n365  click-to-filter needs a real column to key the slicer on.\n366  \n367  **A series narrows its own fact with `where`**, the same clause a measure takes and against the\n368  same declared keys. So an evidence answering *\"TOI by sector by month by scenario\"* is **one**\n369  fact, and lines that differ only by scenario are `where` clauses over it — not one fact each.\n370  A pinned dimension stops following the slicer and whatever the `where` leaves alone keeps\n371  following it — so a line pinned to one scenario holds that scenario whatever the reader\n372  selects, and still narrows when they click a sector.\n373  \n374  `afb_line` draws three lines over one time axis and its shape differs from the other two:\n375  a top-level `months`, then three sibling series, all three required. Each series is **looked\n376  up by period value** — for every entry of `months` the engine takes every row whose `month_col`\n377  prints the same and totals them (`agg` changes how, default `sum`). Row order is irrelevant,\n378  nothing is padded, and a period matching no row is a gap in that line, which is how Actual\n379  stops mid-year and Forecast starts where it stopped. If several rows share a period you get\n380  their total, so `where` down to the one line you mean — a fact holding every sector draws the\n381  sector total, which is right only if that is what the chart is for.\n382  \n383  ```json chart_afb\n384  { \"kind\": \"afb_line\",\n385    \"months\": [\"202601\", \"202602\", \"202603\", \"202604\", \"202605\", \"202606\",\n386               \"202607\", \"202608\", \"202609\", \"202610\", \"202611\", \"202612\"],\n387    \"actual\":   { \"from\": { \"fact\": \"toi\", \"col\": \"value\", \"month_col\": \"rptmonth\",\n388                            \"where\": { \"data_type\": \"act\" } } },\n389    \"forecast\": { \"from\": { \"fact\": \"toi\", \"col\": \"value\", \"month_col\": \"rptmonth\",\n390                            \"where\": { \"data_type\": \"fc\" } } },\n391    \"budget\":   { \"from\": { \"fact\": \"toi\", \"col\": \"value\", \"month_col\": \"rptmonth\",\n392                            \"where\": { \"data_type\": \"bud\" } } },\n393    \"forecast_band\": [\"202607\", \"202612\"] }\n394  ```\n395  \n396  The repetition in that block is the shape, not an oversight: `actual`, `forecast` and `budget`\n397  are three objects and each carries its own `from`. There is no `fact` or `month_col` at the\n398  spec level to lift them to — the card shows none, and hoisting them there fails the build.\n399  The repeated `from` is exactly what lets three lines read one fact under different `where`.\n400  \n401  `months` is also the tick label, so binding a period column by reference puts period codes on\n402  the axis — the price of not retyping the numbers, and the right one. `month_col` defaults to\n403  `month`, which fits only a wide fact that already has one row per month and a column of that\n404  name (`campaign.json`).\n405  \n406  ## Filters, dimensions and conditional formats\n407  \n408  `filters: [ { \"dim\": \"sector\" } ]` builds the slicer and turns on chart cross-filtering.\n409  **A chart axis or pie slice bound to a declared dimension needs that dimension in\n410  `filters[]`** — without slicer state to toggle, the click would silently do nothing, so the\n411  lint rejects it. (If the axis isn't meant to be interactive, don't bind it to a dimension.)\n412  \n413  `conditional_formats` maps values to RAG classes, referenced by name from a KPI `cf`,\n414  progress `rule`, or cell `cf`:\n415  \n416  ```json conditional_format\n417  { \"thresholds\": [ {\"op\":\"lt\",\"value\":85,\"class\":\"bad\"}, {\"op\":\"lt\",\"value\":100,\"class\":\"warn\"} ],\n418    \"else_class\": \"good\" }\n419  ```\n420  \n421  `cf` and `rule` take that **name**, or an inline object — never a `{{token}}`. Which value\n422  gets tested is a separate key, `target`, and its default is the useful case: leave `target`\n423  out and the rule reads whatever field it is attached to, so one declared rule colours twenty\n424  KPI cards each by its own figure. Set `target` only when the value to test is not the value\n425  on display — a card showing tỷ but coloured by achievement percent — and then write the rule\n426  inline, since it is specific to that card. No key names a measure from inside a threshold;\n427  `target` is the only thing that chooses which number.\n428  \n429  ### One column, four places\n430  \n431  `where`, `dimensions`, `filters[]` and a chart axis all touch the same column and do four\n432  different jobs. Nearly every question that *looks* like a report-design choice is answered\n433  here, and answered the same way every time:\n434  \n435  | The column appears in… | Runs | Does | Does **not** do |\n436  |---|---|---|---|\n437  | a fact `where` | before anything is declared, on the raw file | drops non-matching rows for good — nothing downstream sees them | make the column a dimension; the rows are gone, not pinned |\n438  | a measure or series `where` | at render, against declared keys | **pins that one dimension for that one binding** — the slicer for it stops applying *here* | pin the other dimensions; those still follow the slicer |\n439  | `data.dimensions` | build | fixes label and slicer order, and lets a `where` name the column | build a slicer |\n440  | `filters[]` | build | builds the slicer and turns on chart cross-filtering | reach a fact whose rows lack that column |\n441  | a chart `x.col` / pie `name_col` | build | the axis, and the click target | survive as a *declared* dimension that `filters[]` omits — that combination fails the build. An axis on a plain column is fine and simply does not click |\n442  \n443  Two consequences are worth reading rather than deriving, because both are cheap to state and\n444  expensive to prove from the outside:\n445  \n446  **A pinned series does not go blank.** A series carrying `where: {\"sector\": \"re\"}` still shows\n447  Real Estate when the reader selects Infrastructure: for `sector`, the pin wins. So one section\n448  can hold six sector-pinned charts *and* a live `sector` slicer — the charts stay put while\n449  every unpinned binding moves. You never need a fact pre-filtered per member, or a dropped\n450  column, to get that.\n451  \n452  **A column you never mention is never a dimension.** Inference reaches only columns the config\n453  already declares or names — in `filters[]`, a measure `where` key, or a table `dim`. A\n454  `product` column sitting in a fact nobody queries by product stays an ordinary column: no keys,\n455  nothing validated against, and no way to trip the chart lint. Adding a dimension never adds a\n456  failure, so a column you are not slicing on needs no thought at all.\n457  \n458  ### Cross-filtering across breakdowns\n459  \n460  A slicer only reaches a fact whose rows carry that dimension's column, so two breakdowns\n461  co-filter only through a shared column. In order of preference:\n462  \n463  1. **One fact at the intersection grain.** Ask Genie for the metric broken down by *both*\n464     dims in a single query and emit one fact. Every measure aggregates from it, both slicers\n465     cross-filter everything, and totals reconcile.\n466  2. **Stamp the parent key** when grains differ but a hierarchy exists (product → segment):\n467     add the parent key to each child row, and picking a `sector` narrows the product fact.\n468     The reverse stays undone on purpose — a segment total can't be re-derived from one\n469     product without product-grain data.\n470  3. **Keep them independent and say so.** Two disjoint facts are two honest lenses. Note it\n471     rather than invent an intersection Genie never returned.\n472  \n473  Model at the grain you want filtered and ask Genie for that grain. Never synthesize the\n474  intersection in the config.\n475  \n476  ## Sections\n477  \n478  A section's `label` is both the eyebrow above its H2 and its sidebar link — one source, no\n479  `nav` field. `in_nav:false` hides a section; `nav_level:1` nests it.\n480  \n481  ## When build_report says ERROR\n482  \n483  Nothing was written — no report, no half-built file — and the message holds every problem\n484  found, grouped, each with its JSON path and the allowed values at that path. So it is a\n485  punch list, not a diagnosis to make.\n486  \n487  The tool's last line names the repair: `edit_file` on the same `config_path` when the list\n488  names a few keys, a fresh `write_file` when it reaches across most of the config. Either way,\n489  fix **all** the items before calling again — one fix per round trip returns the same list\n490  minus that item. Read the errors literally and group them: `where clause points at an\n491  undeclared dimension: 'scenario'` repeated across 13 measures is one missing declaration in\n492  `data.dimensions`, and *undeclared* means **add** the declaration, never delete the\n493  reference. `edit_file`'s `old_string` must match the file as it stands right now.\n494  \n495  What decides the cost here is not which repair you pick, it is how many times you repair —\n496  and that number is fixed before the first build, by how many key names you took from *The\n497  contract* and how many you supplied yourself.\n498  \n499  **Attempts are not rationed. The shape of the list is the signal.** A build that carries the\n500  fix is worth having on the fourth try as much as on the first, so keep repairing while the\n501  list is getting shorter. When it comes back the same length, or longer, stop editing: that is\n502  one misreading being re-applied, and another edit re-applies it. Go back to *The contract*\n503  for the key those errors keep naming, fix the reading, then fix the config.","duration_ms":0}}
